Event detail uses a known-shape skeleton, distinguishes a confirmed 404 from a transport failure, and offers retry; sermon links reject unsafe/unsupported URLs and surface an inline failure. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **25/25** Jest tests pass. Expo Doctor remains **19/20**, with only the already-documented tracked `.expo` index state; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-shell-recovery-v1` contains successful web, iOS, and Android production bundles. Native QA was retried on the booted iPhone 16 Pro simulator, but the official Expo Go download reached only 2% after roughly 42 minutes and was stopped, so no visual/device claim is made from it.

**Giving ownership and push-lifecycle follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the mobile history contract and gateway now agree: `/finance/me/giving` returns the signed-in member's newest transaction rows rather than a summary object that `FlatList` could not render. Callback settlement now loads the transaction and enforces self-or-leader ownership *before* contacting Paystack; previously any signed-in member who learned another reference could verify and receive that transaction. Digital checkouts store a private, non-serialized `initiatedBy` identity alongside the optional public `memberId`. Therefore “Give anonymously” still removes the giver from church-facing records, while the initiating member can verify the callback, see their own history, receive the transactional receipt, and cannot reset cumulative transfer calculations by toggling anonymity. Existing attributed transactions remain visible through the owner query. The private identity has a tenant-scoped index and is explicitly proven absent from transaction JSON. Already-consented devices now refresh their Expo push-token registration at authenticated app launch without prompting; first consent remains an explicit Notifications-screen action. Finance-domain tests, the complete `internal/service` package, mobile ESLint and TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **28/28** mobile Jest tests pass. The full Kafka receipt test initially exposed a stale-topic race in its own signal channel; it now waits for the transaction created by that run and completes in 2.45s instead of failing on an unrelated historical event and deadlocking cleanup. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-payment-ownership-v1` contains successful web, iOS, and Android bundles. WP-19 still requires the real Paystack/SMS staging proof and remains open.

**Server-enforced OTP follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the registration journey can no longer be bypassed by returning to password sign-in before verifying the new phone. Legacy registration now writes `phoneVerificationRequired: true` only on newly created accounts; the Go password path refuses those accounts with recovery copy directing the member to phone-code sign-in, and successful OTP verification atomically marks the phone verified and clears the requirement before issuing the session. Existing accounts whose documents predate the flag remain grandfathered, avoiding an accidental platform-wide lockout. Registration now checks both email and phone before resolving or creating a church, returns one non-enumerating conflict response, and prevents a duplicate founding-admin phone from leaving an orphan church. The auth-domain integration suite, complete `internal/service` suite, legacy API build and 5/5 API tests, mobile ESLint/TypeScript, 28/28 mobile tests, and `git diff --check` pass. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-server-otp-gate-v1` contains successful web, Android, and iOS bundles. This proves local enforcement and packaging, not SMS delivery; the WP-19 live-phone gate remains open.

**Deferred navigation follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** payment callbacks and notification taps are no longer handed to the auth-only navigator and discarded when the member is signed out. While authentication is active, the app records one allowlisted `altaros://` destination; after OTP/password success it remounts the member navigator, consumes that destination exactly once, and then clears it so a later logout/login cannot replay a stale route. `giving/complete` is explicitly allowlisted so a Paystack return survives authentication, while arbitrary web/admin URLs are rejected. A fresh OS launch URL takes priority over an older stored notification response, preventing stale notification state from redirecting the member away from payment verification. The native last-notification response is explicitly cleared after capture or foreground handling; Expo otherwise persists it and could reopen an already handled destination on a later app start. Authenticated foreground linking continues through React Navigation's normal subscription. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **29/29** Jest tests pass. An export invoked from the monorepo root correctly failed because that directory has no Expo `App.tsx`; authoritative reruns from `apps/mobile` succeeded, with the final web/iOS/Android artifacts at `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-deferred-links-v3`.

**Exact gift-entry follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the mobile giving form no longer converts the member's decimal text through JavaScript floating-point or `toFixed(2)`. It accepts at most two fractional digits while typing and canonicalises valid positive values as exact decimal strings for the quote and charge requests (`10` → `10.00`, `10.9` → `10.90`) without changing their monetary value. Inputs that previously could be silently transformed — including `10.999` → `11.00` — are now rejected, as are exponent notation, multiple decimal points, zero, and negative amounts. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, **42/42** Jest tests, finance-domain tests, and the complete `internal/service` suite pass. Fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles are at `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-exact-money-v1`. This strengthens the local giving path but does not substitute for WP-19's real Paystack/SMS live-phone proof, which remains open.

**Community resilience follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the prayer screen no longer flashes a false empty state while its first network request is still running. Initial prayer loading uses the known-shape skeleton; a failed request has an accessible inline error and retry action; pull-to-refresh reconciles the list without replacing it; and empty content is shown only after a successful empty response. The community feed now preserves its loaded list when focus returns from post creation or comments instead of unnecessarily replacing it with a full-screen skeleton, and failures after cached content or an optimistic reaction are visible with an inline retry rather than being silently hidden by a `ListEmptyComponent` that never renders for non-empty data. New spiritual-service contract tests prove bounded prayer loading, anonymous-choice submission, and encoded dynamic prayer identifiers. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **45/45** Jest tests pass. Fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles are at `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-resilient-community-v1`. These are client resilience improvements against the gateway proxy; they do not advance Phase 2 ownership of the spiritual/social HTTP domains ahead of WP-19.

**Notification and event interaction follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** notification list failures, push-consent failures, mark-read failures, and deep-link failures now have independent state instead of competing for one error string. A failed optimistic mark-read restores the unread state; link and permission lookups cannot reject into an unhandled promise; list failures offer retry both with and without cached notifications; and an action failure is no longer mislabeled as an empty inbox or hidden when data exists. Event cards no longer nest the RSVP button inside the event-detail navigation touch target: details and attendance are separate accessible controls, so one tap cannot mutate the RSVP and open another screen. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **45/45** Jest tests pass. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** solely because the current Git index still tracks historical `.expo` paths even though the ignore rule is correct; the eventual commit must record their already-present deletions. Fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles are at `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-notification-events-v1`. WP-19 remains open for real-provider and live-phone proof.

**Truthful history and welfare-state follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** a failed initial giving-history request no longer renders a confident `GHS 0.00` summary, because unavailable financial data is not evidence of zero giving. A refresh failure over existing records preserves them but labels the total as last loaded, exposes an accessible error, and offers retry instead of silently presenting cached data as current. Welfare request history now has a known-shape, accessibility-labelled skeleton rather than loading prose; pull-to-refresh and retry preserve the urgent-help and request-form surfaces instead of replacing the screen or discarding cached requests. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **45/45** Jest tests pass. Fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles are at `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-truthful-history-welfare-v1`. Expo web also started successfully on a dedicated local port, but the browser runtime exposed no controllable browser instance, so no responsive visual claim is made; responsive/native visual QA remains open alongside WP-19's provider gate.

**Profile, comments, and media resilience follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** comment-list failures and comment-submit failures now have separate state and retry semantics, so a failed send is not mislabeled as an unavailable thread and a list retry cannot masquerade as resubmission. Loaded comments survive focus refreshes; stale-list failures are visible alongside cached content; editing clears the relevant submit error. Sermons now support pull-to-refresh, retain cached messages without reverting to a first-load skeleton, and label refresh failures instead of hiding them when the list is non-empty; bounded sermon retrieval gained contract coverage. Profile privacy/help links check device support, handle launch failure inline, and expose link rather than button semantics. Local and all-device sign-out alert callbacks consume expected network rejection after the context securely clears the local session, preventing unhandled promises during navigator teardown. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **46/46** Jest tests pass. Fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles are at `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-profile-social-media-v1`. WP-19 and visual device QA remain explicitly open.

**Canonical OTP identity follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** phone identity now converges on one E.164 key before registration, OTP request, and OTP verification instead of relying on two client-only string replacements. The mobile boundary accepts common Ghanaian domestic, bare-national, `233…`, `00233…`, formatted `+233…`, and explicit foreign international forms; malformed, too-short, and ambiguous values are rejected before an account can be created with an unusable OTP address. The Go auth boundary independently normalises with the shared launch-market phone package, so a caller cannot bypass canonicalisation by skipping the Expo client; invalid versus missing phone input has a specific safe 400 response. Integration coverage proves that a code requested as `024 123 4567` verifies as `00233 24 123 4567` against the one stored `+233241234567` identity, while unusable input never reaches lookup or SMS delivery. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, **51/51** Jest tests, the auth-domain integration suite, and complete `internal/service` suite pass. Fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles are at `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-canonical-otp-phone-v1`. This proves normalization and local delivery wiring, not a real SMS; WP-19 remains open.

**Compact-device OTP follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the six fixed 48-point OTP fields previously required 376 points after gaps and page padding, overflowing common 320/360-point Android viewports. Code entry now derives field width and gap from the live viewport, caps at the original comfortable tablet/web size, and sits in a keyboard-avoiding scroll container. Deterministic layout tests prove all six fields plus padding fit 320, 360, and 375-point widths. A registration whose first SMS request failed no longer exposes editable code fields or a Verify action before a successful request; after resend, the stale visible code is cleared, the first field is focused, and the copy changes to confirm delivery. Paste/autofill remains on the first field with an explicit accessibility hint. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **56/56** Jest tests pass. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** for the documented historical `.expo` Git-index entries. Fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles are at `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-responsive-otp-v1`. This is deterministic responsive evidence, not a visual device claim; native QA and WP-19 remain open.

**RSVP eligibility and payment re-entry follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** upcoming event lists now remove ended records even while the legacy proxy ignores its `upcoming` query flag. Full and ended events expose a reason and close new RSVP actions in both list and detail views, while an existing attendee can always cancel—even after capacity is reached or the event ends. The giving action now stays locked from quote request through the native review alert and checkout creation; cancelling or dismissing unlocks it, while continuing keeps it locked until Paystack handoff succeeds or fails, preventing duplicate quote/review/payment-initiation flows. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **58/58** Jest tests pass. Fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles are at `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-rsvp-payment-lock-v1`. **Explicit remaining invariant:** the legacy event repository still does not enforce capacity atomically server-side, so the UI is convenience rather than a security/concurrency boundary; that enforcement belongs to WP-21 after WP-19 and is not claimed complete here. WP-19 and device QA remain open.

**Fail-closed session cleanup follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** authoritative refresh rejection now removes the authenticated React tree even if a SecureStore/keychain deletion fails; cleanup attempts every envelope, legacy token, cached-user, and pre-SecureStore key rather than short-circuiting at the first rejection. A non-secret AsyncStorage revocation tombstone is written before deletion and checked before every credential read, so a secure session envelope that survives a device-storage fault cannot silently resurrect after restart. That marker is removed only after a complete replacement token pair is durably stored; legacy-key cleanup cannot turn that successful write into a failed login. The interceptor preserves the gateway's original session error rather than replacing it with a secondary storage failure. Failure-injection tests prove all keys are attempted, expiry listeners still fire, and a deliberately undeletable secure envelope reads back as signed out. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **61/61** Jest tests pass. Fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles are at `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-fail-closed-session-v1`. WP-19 and device QA remain open.

**Mobile route/state audit follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** every mobile service call was checked against the current gateway table and legacy router rather than inferred from screen behavior. Auth, church lookup, finance, and member reads resolve to Go-owned routes; registration and events intentionally fall through the gateway to existing TypeScript routes; social, spiritual, welfare, and member notifications remain client-ready Phase 2 surfaces with no legacy server routes and are not claimed live. The global offline alert now announces connectivity loss assertively to assistive technology. The community composer enforces its advertised 500-character boundary at input time and announces the remaining count, and welfare category controls expose descriptive accessible names. This closes a pre-gate client-boundary/accessibility gap without moving Phase 2 server ownership ahead of WP-19. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **61/61** Jest tests pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-route-state-audit-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS production bundles. WP-19 and native visual QA remain open.

**Rendered responsive auth follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the exported Expo web build was exercised through Chrome's DevTools device emulation rather than judged from bundle success. At real 390×844 and 320×568 CSS viewports the redesigned phone-first sign-in has zero horizontal overflow; the compact registration form also stays within 320 points and its complete 792-point content, including church code and Create Account, remains reachable through the 568-point scroll viewport. At 768 points registration previously stretched edge to edge while sign-in used a deliberate content cap, so registration now shares the 620-point centered auth frame; computed geometry confirms `x=74`, `width=620`, `right=694` with a 768-point viewport. Screenshots were inspected at `/tmp/altar-mobile-emulated-390.png`, `/tmp/altar-mobile-register-320-bottom.png`, and `/tmp/altar-mobile-register-768-final.png`. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **61/61** Jest tests pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-responsive-auth-qa-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. This is meaningful responsive browser evidence, not native-device proof; WP-19 and live-phone QA remain open.

**OTP-to-member rendered-flow follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** a 320×568 DevTools-emulated session now exercises the real exported UI from phone entry through the OTP navigator and into the authenticated home shell. Only network boundaries were intercepted: the request-code response, verification session envelope, and home read models were synthetic and CORS-correct; all input, navigation, session persistence, responsive layout, and rendering remained application code. The request failure path first surfaced its inline retry copy when a deliberately incomplete CORS response was rejected. The success path canonicalised `0241234567` to `+233241234567`, rendered all six fields inside 320 points with zero horizontal overflow, distributed a trusted full-code paste across the fields, kept Verify disabled before completion, and entered Home after a contract-shaped verification response. The first authenticated capture exposed `Community` truncating to `Commun…` in the five-item 320-point tab bar; only its compact tab label is now `Social`, while the route and header remain `Community`. The final capture at `/tmp/altar-mobile-home-320-social-final.png` shows all five untruncated tabs; `/tmp/altar-mobile-otp-320.png` records the OTP layout. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **61/61** Jest tests pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-otp-authenticated-qa-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. This proves responsive client behavior against synthetic boundary responses, not real SMS, Paystack, or native-device delivery; WP-19 remains open.

**Primary-tab and wide-content rendered follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the persisted synthetic member session was used to render every bottom-tab destination at the Android-low-end 320×568 target with contract-shaped read responses. Give, Social, Events, and Profile all have zero page-level horizontal overflow; populated social and event cards remain legible; the complete 1,206-point giving form scrolls through every payment method to the note, anonymous choice, disabled amount action, history link, and Paystack disclosure; the 980-point profile scroll reaches both local and all-device sign-out actions plus the authoritative-record notice. Inspected captures are `/tmp/altar-mobile-tab-give-320.png`, `/tmp/altar-mobile-tab-social-320.png`, `/tmp/altar-mobile-tab-events-320.png`, `/tmp/altar-mobile-tab-profile-320.png`, `/tmp/altar-mobile-give-bottom-320.png`, and `/tmp/altar-mobile-profile-bottom-320.png`. A consistency audit then found Feed, Events, Sermons, Prayer, Welfare, and the devotional body lacked the 680-point reading frame already used elsewhere; all now share it. Exact 768-point browser geometry confirms active Feed and Events content containers at `x=44`, `width=680`, with no body overflow. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **61/61** Jest tests pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-primary-tabs-responsive-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Provider-backed mutations and native-device behavior remain outside this synthetic render proof; WP-19 stays open.

**Secondary-route rendered follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the real Home, Profile, Social, and stack-navigation controls were exercised at 320×568 through Daily Devotional, Sermons, Prayer Requests, Welfare & Support, Event Details, Notifications, Giving History, New Post, and Comments. Every route loaded a populated contract-shaped state, preserved a usable back path, and reported zero page-level horizontal overflow. OTP now shares the 620-point auth frame; event details cap each reading/action section at 680 points while retaining the full-width event masthead; the focused post composer is also centered at 680 points on wider surfaces. Giving and welfare fixtures were rerun after the initial QA fixtures were found to use the wrong typed fields: the corrected finance record renders `GH₵20.00` in both total and row, and the corrected welfare record renders `01/08/2026`, proving there was no app-side NaN/date defect. Captures are under `/tmp/altar-mobile-secondary-*-320.png`, including corrected `/tmp/altar-mobile-secondary-giving-history-correct-320.png` and `/tmp/altar-mobile-secondary-welfare-correct-320.png`. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **61/61** Jest tests pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-secondary-routes-v1` contains web, Android, and iOS bundles. A fresh Expo Doctor run remains **19/20**: both ignore files contain `.expo/`, but the current Git index still tracks the three already-deleted historical `.expo` paths, so the eventual owner commit must record those deletions. WP-19 remains open for native phone and real provider proof.

**Rendered mutation-recovery follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** member actions were exercised through the real rendered controls with stateful synthetic responses rather than inferred from request helpers. RSVP changed Sunday Worship from 84 attending / `RSVP` to 85 / `Cancel RSVP`; prayer changed `Pray (8)` to `Pray (9)`. Notification mark-read failure restored the unread accessibility prefix and showed its specific action error; the next successful attempt removed both. Social reaction failure rolled the optimistic heart/count back from 13 to 12, but the audit found that a later successful retry reached 13 while leaving the obsolete failure banner visible. `FeedScreen` now clears that action error before each new reaction attempt. The exact fail→rollback→success sequence was rerun against the fresh export: the final state is a filled heart with 13 likes and no stale error copy. Giving review/payment concurrency and exact quote acceptance remain covered by the existing service/state tests and were not represented as a provider-backed rendered payment. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **61/61** Jest tests pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-mutation-recovery-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. WP-19 remains open for real settlement, receipt, and native-phone proof.

**WCAG contrast-token follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the rendered palette was audited mathematically instead of accepted by visual inspection. The shared 10–14-point `muted` foreground was only **3.46–3.91:1** across the app's background, card, muted-input, and pale-green surfaces; warning and info status labels also fell as low as **3.75:1** and **4.23:1**. Those semantic tokens are now restrained darker variants (`muted #5B716C`, `warning #965F10`, `info #2F6F80`) that preserve the established green-grey/amber/blue language. The worst recurring muted pair is now **4.62:1**; warning and info both exceed **4.5:1** on all four shared light surfaces and on the exact 9.4%-opacity badge tint used by status pills. A new theme regression suite calculates WCAG relative luminance for normal text, primary links, success/warning/error/info labels, and their badge backgrounds. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **70/70** Jest assertions across 11 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-contrast-safe-v2` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. WP-19 remains open.

**Rendered touch-target follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** actual 320-point DOM rectangles exposed interaction boxes that accessibility labels alone could not prove: Home's All events/sermons actions were 17 points high, giving amount/type pills 31–35, social reaction/comment actions 21, compact RSVP 31, and the phone/password auth tabs 33. The shared Button now enforces a 44-point minimum, while compact visual treatments retain their typography through centered hit areas. Home text actions, giving chips, feed actions, auth method/registration links, OTP resend, notification/history retries, and prayer/welfare chips and text actions received the same floor. A fresh-bundle geometric audit across every visible button, link, tab, checkbox, and radio on Home, Give, Social, Events, Profile, Login, and Register reports no sub-44-point targets; the final auth method tabs measure exactly `132×44` at 320 points. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **70/70** Jest assertions pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-touch-targets-v2` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. WP-19 remains open.

**Mobile trust-boundary follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** a source audit found no mobile `console` logging of session or member data. The remaining unencoded dynamic service paths were the church and event identifiers in the event client; both now encode the complete path segment, with regression coverage for slashes and spaces. The post-review payment handoff now accepts only an absolute HTTPS checkout URL with a hostname and no embedded credentials before asking the operating system to open it; `javascript:`, custom-scheme, HTTP, credentialed, relative, empty, and non-string values are rejected. This protects the real quote-before-create giving flow and does not claim provider completion. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **78/78** Jest assertions across 11 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-safe-boundaries-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. WP-19 remains open for live-phone Paystack, SMS, and staging-infrastructure proof.

**Asynchronous MoMo settlement follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the payment-return screen previously made one settlement request and left an honestly pending mobile-money callback entirely to manual retry. It now performs a bounded sequence of four provider-verification attempts at four-second intervals, stops immediately on `success`, `failed`, or `reversed`, cancels its timer and ignores late state writes when the route unmounts, and prevents overlapping settlement requests. The member sees the current attempt count, an explicit warning not to begin another payment, and a manual safe retry after the bounded window or a transport failure. Pending is never promoted to confirmed giving. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **89/89** Jest assertions across 12 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-momo-settlement-poll-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** only for the already-documented historical `.expo` paths still present in the current Git index despite correct ignore rules. This is deterministic client readiness, not provider proof; WP-19 remains open for a live-phone Paystack/SMS staging run.

**Foreground session-recovery follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** secure-session reconciliation no longer happens only at cold launch. When an authenticated app returns from `inactive` or `background`, it refreshes `/auth/me` before the member takes another action; concurrent resume signals coalesce into one request, the AppState subscription is removed on unmount, and resolved work cannot write into an unmounted provider. Offline/network and gateway 5xx failures preserve the cached member experience, while an authoritative 401, 403, or confirmed missing member (404) clears the secure envelope and authenticated UI. Local keychain-cleanup failure cannot resurrect or visually retain the ended session. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **90/90** Jest assertions across 12 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-foreground-session-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. WP-19 remains open for real-phone authentication, SMS, and provider staging proof.

**Same-frame mutation-safety follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** loading/disabled button state is committed by React after an event handler returns, leaving a same-frame double-tap window in which member mutations could be issued twice. A synchronous lock now closes that window across the giving quote/review/payment sequence, post and comment creation, prayer submission and per-request `Pray`, private welfare submission and emergency confirmation, event RSVP, social reactions, notification permission registration, and notification mark-read/deep-link actions. Independent row actions use keyed locks; single transactional flows use one lock. Locks release on success, transport failure, cancellation, native alert dismissal, and early return, while giving remains locked throughout the quote dialog until cancellation or the payment handoff finishes. Notification mark-read rollback and link-error semantics remain separate. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **93/93** Jest assertions across 13 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-mutation-locks-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. These guards improve client idempotence but do not replace server idempotency or live provider proof; WP-19 remains open.

**Welfare contract-alignment follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** because welfare has no reference implementation and remains WP-27 work, §0.2 requires it to be built from `shared-types`; the mobile client instead had a conflicting speculative model (`summary/details`, `submitted/in_review/closed`, a bereavement-only category, and `/requests/me`). The client-ready boundary now matches the shared Phase 2 case model: one description, the financial/medical/food/housing/counselling/other category set, low/medium/high/critical urgency, explicit anonymity, pending/under-review/approved/fulfilled/declined states, `createdAt`, member history at `/welfare/my-requests`, and emergency context at `/welfare/emergency-alert`. The member form exposes urgency and anonymity as accessible controls, explains that anonymity hides the public/shared case name rather than defeating authorised safety/audit records, and renders urgency/anonymity in history. No character maximum was invented because neither the shared contract nor an implemented gateway validator defines one yet. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **96/96** Jest assertions across 14 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-welfare-contract-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. This proves client/schema alignment only: WP-27 remains Phase 2 and WP-19 remains open.

**Social shared-contract follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** WP-24 also has no reference server and must be built from `shared-types`; the shared/member-web wire model uses `{data,total}`, `authorAvatarUrl`, `isLikedByMe`, and required `general | testimony | praise_report` post types, while mobile assumed `{posts,total}`, `authorAvatar`, `isLiked`, and untyped posts. The mobile adapter now normalises both envelopes and both field-name generations, normalises shared comment arrays, defaults absent legacy type/reaction fields safely, and accepts both a returned reaction count and the shared endpoint's count-less success without undoing the already-correct optimistic count. New posts send the required type, and the composer exposes accessible radio controls for Post, Testimony, and Praise report. Route identifiers remain encoded. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **99/99** Jest assertions across 14 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-social-contract-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. This is backward-compatible client readiness, not a claim that WP-24 routes are live; WP-19 remains open.

**Prayer shared-contract follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the WP-28 shared prayer model and member-web service use `/spiritual/prayer-requests` with title, description, anonymity, author, time, and prayer count; mobile used a different `/spiritual/prayers` family and invented required category/status fields that no shared contract or server implementation promises. The mobile adapter and UI now use the cross-client planned route, send only shared fields, encode request identifiers, accept a shared array or `{data|requests,total}` envelope, and default only an absent numeric prayer count. A count-less successful `Pray` action retains the client-side increment; a returned count remains authoritative. Category controls and badges were removed rather than presenting fabricated metadata. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **101/101** Jest assertions across 14 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-prayer-contract-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. This proves client/schema alignment only: encrypted storage, pastoral ACLs, and live routes remain WP-28 after the WP-19 gate.

**Notification shared-contract follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** member web and `shared-types` define self-scoped notification history as `GET /notifications` with `{success,data}`, read mutation as `PUT /notifications/:id/read`, state through `status/readAt`, and navigation payloads inside `metadata`; mobile assumed `/notifications/me`, POST, a bare array, and a top-level deep link. The mobile adapter now accepts both shared envelopes and the earlier mobile-shaped array, maps `metadata.deepLink|url` into the existing allowlisted navigation boundary, maps a shared `READ` status to a stable read timestamp when `readAt` is absent, and uses the encoded shared PUT route. Mobile-specific Expo device registration remains isolated and unchanged. Existing optimistic rollback and separate deep-link error handling remain intact. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **104/104** Jest assertions across 14 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-notification-contract-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. This is a client contract, not a live notification-service claim; WP-22 remains Phase 2 and WP-19 remains open.

**Spiritual-content shared-contract follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** shared devotionals use `scripture` for the reference and `scriptureText` for the quoted passage, while the earlier mobile shape used `scripture` for the passage and `scriptureReference` for the reference; consuming the shared object directly would quote only “Ephesians 2:8” and render no citation. The adapter now maps both generations into one reading model, and devotional/sermon collections accept arrays plus `{data|devotionals|sermons,total}` envelopes. The existing today endpoint remains unchanged because neither `shared-types` nor WP-28 specifies a replacement. Valid dates render as member-facing Ghana-formatted dates rather than raw ISO timestamps. Sermon handoff now requires an absolute credential-free HTTPS URL and surfaces an inline secure-link error for HTTP, custom-scheme, credentialed, relative, or malformed media instead of handing it to the OS. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **114/114** Jest assertions across 14 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-spiritual-content-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. This remains WP-28 client readiness; licensing, offline Bible sync, live routes, and WP-19 provider/device proof remain open.

**Truthful event-model and ordering follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the event adapter was checked against the actual legacy controller/repository and shared `Event`/`RSVP` types. Its route, tenant check, envelope, and RSVP status calls were correct, but mobile fabricated category, organiser, address, and image fields and displayed `other` / `Your church` as server facts. Those fields and badges are removed; absent description now receives clearly generic UI copy instead of invented domain metadata. The client-only `upcoming` flag is stripped before the request because the repository ignores it. Fetched upcoming events are filtered and sorted earliest-first; Home requests a bounded 50-event candidate set before selecting its nearest requested count, avoiding the previous “five farthest future events” result from the backend's descending sort. This is still bounded client compensation, not a substitute for WP-21 adding a server-side future-date query. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **116/116** Jest assertions across 14 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-event-truth-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. WP-19 remains open.

**Known-offline request-boundary follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the global banner previously announced connectivity loss but every screen still started its Axios request and waited for the transport timeout or failure. The shared mobile API interceptor now queries the current NetInfo snapshot before every gateway call and rejects immediately with safe reconnect copy when either connection or internet reachability is definitively false. Unknown reachability and a failed NetInfo lookup remain permissive, so startup, captive-network transitions, and platforms that cannot determine reachability are not falsely blocked. This one boundary covers authentication/OTP, session reconciliation, home reads, giving/settlement/history, RSVP, social, spiritual, welfare, notifications, and profile actions while preserving each screen's existing draft, optimistic rollback, cached-session, and retry behavior. The banner uses the same predicate. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **120/120** Jest assertions across 15 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-offline-boundary-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** solely for the already-documented `.expo` paths still present in the current Git index despite correct ignore rules. This is fail-fast offline UX, not WP-45 offline data sync; WP-19 remains open.

**Local-first session-exit follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** profile copy and comments claimed sign-out was a local security action first, but both AuthContext actions kept the authenticated tree visible while awaiting `/auth/logout` or `/auth/logout-all`, and `authService` deferred secure-store cleanup until the request completed or timed out. The provider now removes private UI immediately. The service captures the current bearer token into the remote request config, then runs gateway revocation and fail-closed local credential cleanup concurrently; remote/offline failure cannot prevent local deletion, and explicit authentication survives the cleanup race long enough for best-effort server revocation. Both destructive profile confirmations use synchronous locks and release them on cancel, native dismissal, resolution, or rejection, preventing stacked dialogs and duplicate revocations. Tests prove local cleanup begins while a deliberately unresolved remote request is still pending and still runs when revocation rejects. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **122/122** Jest assertions across 15 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-local-first-logout-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. WP-19 remains open.

**Post-contract visual-QA availability check — 1 Aug 2026:** the latest exported web bundle was served locally for a fresh 320-point pass over the changed welfare, social composer, prayer, event, and profile routes, but the supported in-app browser runtime reported no available browser instances after its documented discovery check. The booted iOS 18.5 iPhone 16 Pro simulator was also inspected and still has neither Expo Go nor an Altar OS development client installed. No screenshot, geometry, or native-interaction claim is made from this attempt, and the local server was stopped cleanly. Earlier rendered audits remain evidence for their exact bundles only; the post-contract surfaces still require a current controllable browser or native client. This is an environment limitation, not evidence of a UI failure, and it does not close WP-19.

**Bounded session-refresh follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** ordinary gateway calls already had a 15-second transport timeout, but the bare Axios request used to rotate an expired access token had no timeout. Because every simultaneous `401` intentionally waits on one shared refresh promise, a stalled refresh could suspend all authenticated work indefinitely. Rotation now lives behind an explicit session-refresh boundary that preserves the non-recursive bare client, applies the same definitive-offline preflight as other requests, caps the request at 15 seconds, validates both returned tokens before writing either, and persists the pair atomically through the existing secure-session envelope. A timeout or known-offline result remains a recoverable transport failure and does not erase the cached member session; authoritative `401/403` handling is unchanged. Tests prove the timeout configuration, offline no-send behavior, full-pair persistence, and malformed-response rejection. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **125/125** Jest assertions across 16 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-bounded-refresh-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** only because the three already-deleted `.expo` paths remain in Git's current index; the ignore rules and earlier limitation are unchanged. WP-19 remains open.

**Foreground-notification delivery follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** Expo requires an application notification handler to present an alert while the app is foregrounded; without one, a giving receipt, event reminder, or pastoral message had no declared banner/list behavior while the member was actively using Altar OS. The handler is now installed before the first React render and explicitly shows foreground deliveries in both banner and notification list, plays the configured sound, and declines to invent an application badge count that the current Phase 2 server does not reconcile. Android's stable `default` church-updates channel now carries a description, sound, vibration, light, and default importance, and is recreated idempotently for already-consented registration syncs as well as the first permission flow because OS channel state can change independently of permission. Tests prove the foreground behavior, badge decision, Android channel contract, and non-Android no-op. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **128/128** Jest assertions across 16 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-foreground-notifications-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** for the unchanged historical `.expo` Git-index entries. This proves application-side presentation policy, not live FCM/Expo delivery or notification-service routes; WP-19 remains open.

**Authentication transaction-safety follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** login, initial OTP request, registration, OTP verification, and resend previously depended only on React loading state, which does not disable a control until the next render; a same-frame double tap could issue duplicate requests, and Verify could race Resend against different codes. Each form now acquires a synchronous transaction lock before network work. OTP verify and resend share one lock, all locks release on validation failure, success, or rejection, and fields plus alternate navigation/method controls are disabled while their transaction owns the form. Registration inputs now declare native name, email, telephone, and new-password autofill semantics, with editing frozen during account creation. The shared input component preserves caller accessibility state, marks invalid fields semantically, includes the current validation error in the control hint, and exposes visible errors as polite alerts. The existing lock regression suite proves same-frame exclusion and safe release; the full authentication/service suite remains green. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **128/128** Jest assertions across 16 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-auth-transaction-safety-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** for the unchanged historical `.expo` Git-index entries. Native password-manager and SMS-provider behavior still require real-device proof; WP-19 remains open.

**Money-contract validation follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the review screen previously trusted any quote-shaped gateway JSON and could display or forward negative, fractional, wrong-currency, inconsistent, or JavaScript-unsafe minor units as member consent. User-entered major units are now rejected when their pesewa value exceeds JavaScript's exact-integer range. Both the non-mutating quote response and the quote echoed by checkout creation must contain safe non-negative integer minor units in GHS, a real exemption flag, a non-empty explanation, and an exact `total = intended gift + levy`; otherwise the payment flow stops before opening Paystack with safe error copy. The Go service remains authoritative and independently re-quotes against cumulative daily giving, but the member UI no longer treats malformed transport data as consent. Regression cases cover oversized input, negative and fractional values, wrong currency, blank reasoning, inconsistent totals, and malformed checkout results. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **135/135** Jest assertions across 16 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-money-contract-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** for the unchanged historical `.expo` Git-index entries. This is client trust-boundary evidence, not a live Paystack charge or settlement; WP-19 remains open.

**Giving-record contract follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** settlement, transaction lookup, checkout creation, and member history previously cast gateway JSON directly to `GivingRecord`; TypeScript could not stop an unknown status, NaN/fractional/negative minor units, invalid dates, wrong currency, or expense row from reaching confirmed totals, status styling, currency formatting, and receipt copy. A single runtime normalizer now validates every UI-consumed transaction field: non-empty identity and church, member-giving type, truthful channel, positive safe-integer gross, non-negative safe-integer levy/net, GHS, one of the four lifecycle states, and valid occurrence/creation dates. A malformed list envelope or any invalid record becomes the existing recoverable history/settlement error state rather than false emptiness, a crash, or a misleading amount. The authoritative Go model also showed that a church-recorded cash gift can belong in member history; history now accepts and labels `cash`, while the member-started checkout type remains restricted to the four digital rails. Regression cases cover cash history, unknown/expense types, unknown status, unsafe money, wrong currency, bad dates, and non-array history. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **143/143** Jest assertions across 16 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-giving-record-contract-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** for the unchanged historical `.expo` Git-index entries. Provider settlement and receipt delivery still require WP-19's live staging run.

**Member-identity integrity follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** cold-start restore previously used `session.getUser<User>()`, which was only a JSON cast; any parsed truthy object could mount the authenticated navigator while offline. Gateway authentication also treated any truthy access/refresh values as tokens, and the secure envelope reader accepted truthy non-string JSON. One auth normalizer now guards login, OTP verification, `/auth/me`, and cached identity: the payload must be a non-array object with a non-empty string ID, and every supplied profile field must have its declared string type. Authentication envelopes require a complete pair of non-empty string tokens before either credentials or identity are persisted. Cached objects pass through the same validator before private navigation is committed; malformed cached identity enters the existing fail-closed startup cleanup, while malformed token envelopes are deleted and never attached to requests. Stable display fallbacks for legitimately absent optional name/email/phone fields remain. Regression cases cover null/empty/partial identities, wrong-typed profile data, numeric/blank tokens, invalid cached objects, and truthy non-string secure envelopes. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **152/152** Jest assertions across 16 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-identity-integrity-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** for the unchanged historical `.expo` Git-index entries. WP-19 remains open.

**Pending-session recovery follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** a valid secure token pair can survive while its cached profile is absent—for example after a partial legacy migration or a safely discarded malformed JSON cache. If that app launched offline, the first `/auth/me` reconciliation correctly preserved credentials but mounted no private UI; later foreground recovery was skipped because the old condition looked only for an already-mounted user, leaving the member on sign-in until restart. `AuthContext` now tracks credential presence separately from validated identity. Foreground transitions reconcile any existing session, and NetInfo recovery retries a token-backed identity only when no user is mounted and connectivity is not definitively offline. Both paths share the existing single-flight reconciliation promise, so an initial request, connectivity callback, and foreground event cannot replay rotating credentials concurrently. Authoritative `401/403/404`, local/all-device logout, and expiry clear the session-presence marker; network/5xx failure keeps it pending. Policy tests cover online recovery, unknown reachability, definitive offline signals, no-session state, and already-restored users. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **158/158** Jest assertions across 17 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-pending-session-recovery-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** for the unchanged historical `.expo` Git-index entries. WP-19 remains open.

**Event and RSVP integrity follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** event list/detail payloads were previously cast at runtime, discarded the shared contract's `churchId`, and trusted dates/counts/capacity enough to drive sorting and RSVP availability. RSVP mutation UI also inferred confirmation from the action attempted rather than validating the server's returned event and status. The event boundary now requires non-empty IDs/church/title, the requested church on every church-list row, valid chronological start/end dates, non-negative safe-integer attendance, optional positive safe-integer capacity, correctly typed optional copy, and unique list IDs before FlatList sees them. Detail responses must echo the requested event ID. RSVP history is restricted to requested IDs and known statuses; RSVP/cancel mutations must echo both the exact event and expected `GOING`/`NOT_GOING` state before counts or buttons change. Missing location remains explicit UI fallback copy, not fabricated venue data. Regression cases cover cross-church rows, blank titles, reversed dates, negative counts, fractional capacity, duplicate keys, and mismatched RSVP mutation echoes. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **165/165** Jest assertions across 17 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-event-integrity-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** for the unchanged historical `.expo` Git-index entries. Server-side atomic capacity enforcement remains WP-21 after the WP-19 gate; WP-19 stays open.

**Community integrity follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the compatibility adapter normalised shared and earlier avatar/reaction field names but still cast the rest of each post/comment, discarded shared `churchId`, defaulted a missing collection to empty, and accepted any numeric reaction count as authoritative after optimistic UI. The feed now requires the signed-in member's church and validates every post against it, preserving `churchId` through the app. Posts require stable non-empty identity/content/author, a known or legacy-absent post type, safe non-negative integer like/comment counts, boolean reaction state, valid dates, correctly typed optional media fields, unique IDs, and a sane page total. Comments must belong to the requested post and carry valid identity/content/author/date; duplicate IDs and invalid totals fail into the existing recoverable state. Count-less shared reaction success remains supported, but a returned count must be a safe non-negative integer before replacing the optimistic value. Created posts must also echo the member's church. Regression cases cover cross-church posts, blank content, unknown type, negative/NaN/fractional counts, invalid dates, wrong-post comments, and duplicate keys. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **175/175** Jest assertions across 17 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-social-integrity-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** for the unchanged historical `.expo` Git-index entries. This remains WP-24 client readiness, not a live-route claim; WP-19 stays open.

**Notification-inbox integrity follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the shared notification model is recipient- and church-scoped, but mobile previously discarded both ownership fields, defaulted a missing list payload to a truthful-looking empty inbox, accepted arbitrary status/date/metadata values, and retained external links until tap time. Shared `{success,data}` responses now require `success: true`, a real array, and records whose `churchId` and `recipientId` exactly match the signed-in member. Shared records also require known channel/type/status, non-empty ID/title/body, valid created/sent/read dates, object metadata, and unique IDs. The explicitly supported earlier bare-array shape remains isolated behind the same core content/date checks without pretending it carries shared ownership fields. Metadata/top-level destinations are passed through the existing `altaros://` route allowlist during normalization, so external or malformed links never enter inbox state. Invalid envelopes fail into the existing unavailable/retry UI rather than false emptiness. Empty Expo push tokens are refused before registration. Regression cases cover malformed records, unknown status, invalid dates/metadata, cross-member/church ownership, external URLs, missing collections, duplicate IDs, and empty device tokens. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **184/184** Jest assertions across 17 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-notification-integrity-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** for the unchanged historical `.expo` Git-index entries. This remains WP-22 client readiness, not live delivery evidence; WP-19 stays open.

**Spiritual and prayer integrity follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** shared devotionals, sermons, and prayer requests are church-scoped, and prayers also retain a private member owner, but the mobile reading models discarded those boundaries and cast dates/counts/collections directly. Home plus each spiritual screen now supplies the signed-in church to its service boundary. Devotionals require matching church, stable identity/title, both scripture passage and reference across either supported field generation, content, author, valid date, and typed optional imagery. Sermons require matching church, stable identity/title/speaker, valid date, duration, description, and typed optional media/series fields. Prayer rows require matching church, private member identity, title/description/anonymity, valid creation time, and a safe non-negative integer count; a newly created request must echo the submitting member even when its public display is anonymous. Collections must exist, contain unique IDs, and report sane totals. Count-less Pray success remains supported, but returned counts must be safe integers before replacing the optimistic increment. Existing HTTPS-only sermon handoff remains. Regression cases cover cross-church content, unsafe prayer counts, cross-member created prayers, and missing collections. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **190/190** Jest assertions across 17 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-spiritual-integrity-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** for the unchanged historical `.expo` Git-index entries. This remains WP-28 client readiness; encrypted pastoral storage/ACLs and live routes remain after WP-19, which stays open.

**Welfare integrity follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** welfare history and created cases previously discarded shared `churchId`/`memberId`, trusted category/urgency/status/date values at runtime, and treated any successful HTTP response from the emergency endpoint as proof the pastoral team was notified. The screen now supplies its validated member/church identity to the service. Every private case must echo both owners, carry a known shared category, non-empty description, known urgency/status, real anonymity boolean, valid date, and unique ID before it enters history or status styling. A created case must satisfy the same ownership boundary even when its shared display is anonymous. Missing/non-array or duplicate history fails into the existing private-history unavailable/retry state rather than “no requests.” Emergency context is trimmed, and the UI only shows “Alert sent” after an explicit `{success:true}` acknowledgement; false or malformed acknowledgement takes the existing safety-first failure path that directs immediate danger to local emergency services. Regression cases cover cross-church/member cases, unknown enums, empty description, invalid dates, malformed history, and negative emergency acknowledgement. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **199/199** Jest assertions across 17 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-welfare-integrity-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** for the unchanged historical `.expo` Git-index entries. This remains WP-27 client readiness; pastoral ACL/storage and live routes remain after WP-19, which stays open.

**Authenticated deep-link boundary follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** cold-start links and notification destinations already passed through the mobile route allowlist, but a URL delivered while the authenticated app was open was forwarded directly from React Native Linking to Navigation. The live subscription now uses the same validator before Navigation sees the URL. The validator no longer treats any suffix beneath an allowed prefix as trusted: static routes must match exactly, and only the configured `events/:eventId` and `community/posts/:postId` shapes accept one bounded URL-safe identifier. Control/whitespace characters, encoded separators, malformed encoding, extra path depth, unsafe IDs, and payloads above 2,048 characters are rejected while supported query-bearing payment callbacks remain valid. Regression cases cover prefix-smuggled profile/history routes, nested event routes, encoded slash/space IDs, newline injection, identifier bounds, oversized callbacks, and the valid post-composer route. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **201/201** Jest assertions across 17 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-deep-link-boundary-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** solely for the unchanged historical `.expo` paths still present in the current Git index despite correct ignore rules. This is client navigation-boundary evidence, not live notification/provider proof; WP-19 remains open.

**Member/church identity follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** every private mobile feature is tenant-scoped, but the runtime user normalizer still allowed a truthy identity with no `churchId` to mount the member navigator, leaving home and all domain calls without an enforceable tenant. A non-empty church owner is now mandatory for login, OTP, `/auth/me`, and cached-session identities, and roles are restricted to the gateway's known account roles before private navigation can mount. The Go user contract intentionally carries a church ID rather than duplicating church display data, so validated sessions now enrich home/profile copy through the authenticated `/churches/current` route. That response must echo the exact signed-in church, provide a non-empty name, and not declare the church inactive before it is cached. Newly issued login/OTP tokens are passed explicitly to this secondary request because SecureStore persistence has not completed yet; normal profile reconciliation uses the stored bearer. A failed secondary display lookup does not turn valid authentication into a false sign-in failure—the UI keeps its honest generic church fallback and retries on later reconciliation—while mismatched data is never accepted. Regression cases cover missing church ownership, unknown roles, cross-church enrichment, inactive/blank church data, cache validation, current-profile persistence, and pre-persistence bearer use. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **206/206** Jest assertions across 17 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-member-church-identity-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** solely for the unchanged historical `.expo` paths still present in the current Git index despite correct ignore rules. WP-19 remains open for real-phone Paystack/SMS staging proof.

**Shared accessibility resilience follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the reusable Input built the right merged error hint, disabled state, and focus/blur callbacks, but then spread caller props last, allowing any screen-supplied `accessibilityHint`, `accessibilityState`, `onFocus`, or `onBlur` to silently erase those guarantees. Caller props now enter first and the primitive's composed behavior is applied last: caller callbacks still run, caller accessibility flags survive, disabled state cannot be accidentally cleared, visible validation copy remains in the control hint, and focus styling always updates. Shared avatars now trim whitespace into stable initials, give unnamed profiles an honest `M` fallback, accept only bounded HTTP(S) image URLs without embedded credentials/control characters, and replace failed images with initials instead of leaving a blank face. Both loaded and fallback avatars expose one labelled image element while their decorative initials are hidden from duplicate screen-reader traversal. Regression cases cover merged busy/selected/disabled state, composed validation hints, irregular/empty names, unsupported/local/data URLs, credential-bearing URLs, and oversized image sources. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **210/210** Jest assertions across 18 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-shared-accessibility-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** solely for the unchanged historical `.expo` paths still present in the current Git index despite correct ignore rules. This is deterministic accessibility/runtime evidence; native VoiceOver/TalkBack QA and WP-19's real-phone provider run remain open.

**Public church-code onboarding follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the member registration client called `/churches/slug/:slug` before authentication, but the Go gateway had no such route and placed every church endpoint behind bearer authentication, so a new installation could never resolve its visible church code against the target gateway. The gateway now exposes one intentionally narrow public resolver for active church slugs and returns only opaque church ID, display name, and slug—never organization hierarchy, address/contact data, payout configuration, plan, or settings. Slugs are normalized, bounded, and restricted to the generated lowercase-hyphen grammar; inactive tenants resolve identically to missing tenants so old printed codes cannot admit new members or reveal closure state. The mobile client validates the envelope, non-empty ID/name, and exact requested slug before sending `churchId` to registration; malformed or cross-slug responses stop before account creation. Newly authenticated `/churches/current` remains protected and unchanged. Race-enabled Go church-domain and HTTP-service suites pass, as do mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **211/211** Jest assertions across 18 suites. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-public-church-code-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** solely for the unchanged historical `.expo` Git-index paths. This makes the client/server onboarding contract executable; actual SMS delivery and a full real-phone signup remain inside WP-19's open staging gate.

**Confirmed-church registration UX follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** a valid slug resolver made registration executable, but the form still resolved and created the account in one action, so a typo that happened to match another active branch gave the member no chance to see where they were joining. Registration is now an explicit two-step journey: `Find church` validates the bounded slug and displays the resolved branch name/code in a confirmation card; `Create account` remains disabled until that exact code is confirmed. Editing the code immediately clears confirmation. Submission re-resolves the slug and requires its opaque church ID to match the member-confirmed ID before account creation, preventing a stale or administratively remapped slug from silently switching tenants between confirmation and submit. Discovery failures stay inline on the church field, all relevant inputs/actions freeze during their owning request, and separate synchronous locks prevent duplicate resolution or registration taps. Regression cases cover malformed preflight codes with no request, exact descriptor validation, normal confirmed registration, and changed-ID refusal before POST. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **213/213** Jest assertions across 18 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-church-confirmation-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. A fresh rendered responsive capture was attempted, but no in-app or Chrome browser session was available; the existing 320/390/768 auth captures remain the latest visual evidence, so no new screenshot claim is made for this conditional card. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** solely for the unchanged historical `.expo` Git-index paths. WP-19 remains open for real-phone signup and SMS proof.

**Go auth-envelope and OTP acknowledgement follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the gateway consistently wraps successful data in `{success:true,data}`, but mobile only unwrapped registration; password login, OTP verification, `/auth/me`, and rotating refresh therefore rejected valid Go responses or looked for tokens at the wrong level. One validated auth boundary now accepts both the canonical Go envelope and the explicitly supported earlier raw shape, requires `success:true` whenever an envelope flag exists, and only persists complete non-blank token pairs plus a validated tenant-owned member. OTP request/resend likewise requires a non-empty acknowledgement rather than treating any malformed 200 body as dispatch proof. The phone-login journey now mirrors the gateway's congregation-membership anti-enumeration policy: it says a code was sent *if* the number belongs to a member account, while post-registration verification can truthfully use definite delivery copy. Refresh accepts the Go `{data:{user,tokens}}` result, ignores the extra user safely, and rejects null/array/false/malformed envelopes before touching SecureStore. The gateway's `/auth/me` now also refuses a deactivated account immediately; foreground reconciliation no longer leaves a deactivated member visible until their refresh token rotates. Race-enabled Go auth-domain and HTTP-service suites pass, as do mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **216/216** Jest assertions across 18 suites. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-go-auth-envelope-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** solely for the unchanged historical `.expo` Git-index paths. This proves the client/server auth contract deterministically; WP-19 remains open for real SMS receipt and device verification.

**Cross-domain response-envelope follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** auth exposed a wider strangler-migration fault: Go finance always emits `{success:true,data}`, the proxied event API uses an earlier `{data,pagination}` envelope without a success flag, and other legacy routes mix raw payloads with shared envelopes. Giving quote/checkout/history/transaction/settlement, events/RSVPs, social feed/posts/reactions/comments, devotionals/sermons/prayers, and welfare history/create now converge through one strict success-envelope boundary before their existing money, tenant, identity, date, enum, and count normalizers run. Canonical envelopes must explicitly say `success:true` and contain `data`; false or data-less envelope-shaped 200 bodies fail into each screen's recoverable error state instead of becoming false emptiness, a malformed record, or a payment consent surface. Raw legacy payloads remain supported deliberately, and the event adapter alone retains its documented flag-less `{data,pagination}` compatibility shape. Pagination totals are now also safe non-negative integers at least as large as the returned event page. Regression evidence includes canonical Go finance quote/history/settlement bodies, canonical future/proxied social/spiritual/welfare mutations, canonical RSVP, raw legacy data, the event compatibility envelope, false/data-less envelope refusal, and pagination extraction. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **219/219** Jest assertions across 19 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-domain-envelopes-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** solely for the unchanged historical `.expo` Git-index paths. This proves client boundary compatibility, not live Phase 2 routes; WP-19 and its provider/device gate remain open.

**Checkout handoff integrity follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** after quote acceptance, mobile validated only the echoed levy and base transaction shape; an otherwise well-formed checkout could belong to another church/member, represent a different amount/type/channel, already claim success, omit the SDK access code, or redirect the device to any HTTPS host. Checkout normalization now binds the result to the signed-in church and member, the exact canonical gift amount/currency/type/channel, the member-accepted total, and the only truthful initial status (`pending`) before a payment app/browser can open. Anonymous checkout records must omit the public member owner while the authenticated initiator remains server-side. Redirects are bounded, control-character-free, credential-free HTTPS URLs on exactly `checkout.paystack.com` with no custom port; generic HTTPS, lookalike paths, embedded credentials, and alternate ports are refused. A non-empty idempotency key is now required on every ledger row, and optional provider references/member IDs must be real non-empty strings when present. The service returns the canonicalized safe redirect, and the screen refuses giving when its authenticated identity lacks tenant ownership. Regression cases cover cross-church and anonymous ownership, premature success, malicious checkout host, custom port, missing/malformed quote, and normal pending Paystack handoff. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **223/223** Jest assertions across 19 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-checkout-integrity-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** solely for the unchanged historical `.expo` Git-index paths. This is client/provider-handoff integrity, not a live charge; WP-19 remains open for real Paystack test credentials and phone proof.

**Settlement callback ownership follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** a Paystack return reference is bearer-like data and must not let one signed-in member inspect or actively verify another member's charge. The gateway now privately records the authenticated initiator on every digital checkout, including publicly anonymous gifts, omits that field from JSON, and authorizes transaction lookup and callback settlement against that initiator before contacting the provider; church leaders retain their existing ledger access. Mobile accepts only the finance service's exact `alt_` plus base32 reference grammar, supplies its validated member/church identity to the settlement boundary, and requires the response to echo the exact reference and church. Identified gifts must also echo the signed-in member; anonymous gifts may omit the public member owner because the gateway has already enforced the private initiator. Malformed deep-link references stop before an HTTP request, and a valid-looking cross-member, cross-church, or substituted-reference response is never rendered as payment success. The complete Go suite, race-enabled finance/service suites, mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **225/225** Jest assertions across 19 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-settlement-ownership-v2` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** solely for the unchanged historical `.expo` Git-index paths. Deterministic settlement ownership is closed; WP-19 remains open for a real-phone Paystack charge, webhook, and SMS receipt run.

**Gateway member-registration follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the confirmed church-code journey still posted account creation to `/auth/register`, but the Go gateway had never registered that route, so a fresh install reached a deterministic 404 after the member confirmed their branch. The gateway now creates an account only inside an existing active church, canonicalizes Ghana phone numbers and email, bounds name/password input, assigns exactly `MEMBER`, stores an unverified-phone requirement, and collapses email/phone collisions—including duplicate-key races—into one non-enumerating conflict. Registration deliberately returns no access or refresh token: account creation is not proof of phone control, and OTP verification is the first operation allowed to issue a session. Mobile now validates the registration-only response independently of login, requires the server to echo the confirmed church, canonical phone, and member role, persists no registration credentials, then requests OTP as before. A real MongoDB/Redis integration test proves password login is refused before verification and that the delivered one-time code is the first path to tokens; unavailable tenants and duplicate identity are also covered. The complete Go suite, race-enabled auth/service suites, mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **226/226** Jest assertions across 19 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-gateway-registration-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. All generated `.expo` state is now deleted from the worktree and already covered by ignore rules; Expo Doctor remains **19/20** until those historical tracked deletions are recorded in Git. WP-19 remains explicitly open for live SMS delivery, real-phone signup/OTP, and the Paystack staging run.

**Upcoming-event pagination follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** events are the one post-core member domain currently registered in the legacy API and reachable through the Go gateway proxy; the route audit reconfirmed that social, spiritual, welfare, and member-notification routes remain client-ready Phase 2 work after the WP-19 gate. The live event path previously fetched one newest-first page across past and future rows, then applied `upcoming` and chronological sorting only on-device. Enough old or far-future events could therefore make the home screen report no upcoming gathering while a nearer valid event existed outside that arbitrary page. The authenticated event route now validates bounded pagination, explicit boolean upcoming state, and sort direction; Mongo filters by `endDate >= now` before both count and pagination, orders the filtered set ascending for member surfaces, and carries a matching compound index. Mobile sends that contract explicitly and retains defensive tenant/date validation and sorting. API regressions prove that string `false` is not coerced truthy, malformed/unbounded queries are refused, and the repository filter is applied only for upcoming requests; mobile regressions prove the real query and nearest-event selection. API lint now has zero errors (seven pre-existing console warnings), its TypeScript build and **12/12** Vitest assertions pass, as do the complete Go suite, mobile ESLint/TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **226/226** mobile Jest assertions across 19 suites. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-upcoming-events-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** pending the historical `.expo` deletions being recorded in Git. Atomic capacity enforcement remains owned by WP-21 after the live WP-19 provider/device gate; that gate remains open.

**Compact-auth responsive follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** rendered QA was retried through the supported local browser workflow with Expo web running on port 8083, but the environment exposed no controllable browser instance; the server was stopped cleanly and no screenshot or rendered-viewport claim is made. A source-level compact-width audit still found one deterministic defect: registration forced first and last name into two columns at every width, leaving roughly 130 points per field on a 320-point phone before large-text scaling. Name inputs now stack below 480 points and retain the two-column treatment only on wider devices. Authentication footers and profile identity facts wrap under enlarged text, and long giving amounts shrink within their record row instead of pushing content outside the viewport. Pure layout regressions cover 320, 360, 390, 430, 479, 480, and 768-point widths plus invalid measurements.
