Deterministic responsive behavior improved, but rendered browser/native-device QA and WP-19's real-phone SMS/Paystack run both remain explicitly open.

**Offline mutation-state follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the API interceptor already refused every request when NetInfo definitively reported no connection, but visible member actions stayed enabled and entered brief loading/error cycles despite the global offline banner. One shared hook now projects the exact same conservative policy into the UI: `false` connectivity or reachability blocks, while unknown reachability remains usable. Starting Paystack giving, changing an RSVP, liking or publishing community content, sending a comment, sharing or reacting to prayer, submitting private welfare/care, sending an internet-dependent emergency alert, and registering push notifications are disabled while known offline. Each shared button carries a specific reconnect hint for assistive technology, and custom touch targets expose matching disabled state. The request interceptor remains the race-safe backstop if connectivity drops after render. Unread notification rows deliberately remain navigable to an allowlisted local destination while offline, but are not optimistically or remotely marked read until connectivity returns. Regression evidence covers definitive versus unknown network state and verifies shared buttons preserve the offline explanation alongside disabled/busy accessibility state. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, and **234/234** Jest assertions across 20 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-offline-actions-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** pending the historical `.expo` deletions being recorded in Git. This is deterministic device-state behavior; flight-mode native QA and WP-19's live-phone provider/SMS run remain open.

**Offline onboarding follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the authenticated mutation controls now matched the global banner, but signed-out network actions did not. Church-code lookup, account creation, phone-code dispatch, password login, OTP verification, and OTP resend now use the same definitive-offline state and expose action-specific reconnect hints. Preparation remains intentionally available: members can type or correct their details, switch login method, enter an already-received code, and move between sign-in and registration while offline; only actions that require the gateway are disabled. The existing API interceptor remains the final guard if the connection changes after render, and unknown reachability is still allowed so an inconclusive OS probe cannot lock a member out. **Native push-identity follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the notification client previously treated any non-empty `EXPO_PUBLIC_EAS_PROJECT_ID` as usable, so a placeholder or typo survived export and failed only after the member had granted OS notification permission. Push identity now requires an actual UUID-shaped EAS project ID, trims and canonicalizes it, and refuses a misconfigured build before Android channel creation or the permission prompt. Already-consented token refresh retains its no-prompt behavior and uses the same validated identity. Regression cases cover uppercase/whitespace normalization, placeholder and empty rejection, valid token acquisition, and proof that a misconfigured build never requests consent. `expo install --check` reports dependencies up to date. The resolved public Expo config proves SDK 57, `altaros` scheme, version 1.0.0, `com.altaros.app` on iOS and Android, the notification/SecureStore plugins, portrait phone orientation, and tablet support. **Native push transport correction — 1 Aug 2026 (supersedes the immediately preceding token-identity implementation):** a deeper end-to-end audit found that the authoritative Go adapter sends directly through Firebase Cloud Messaging v1, while `getExpoPushTokenAsync` produces an Expo-relay token that FCM cannot address. Mobile now acquires the native device token, rejects Expo relay/web tokens, rejects a token family that does not match the running OS, bounds and sanitizes the token, and posts `{token, platform: "android" | "ios"}`. Consequently an EAS project UUID is no longer duplicated as a public runtime requirement for token acquisition; EAS project linking remains an external native-build gate. Android's native token matches the existing FCM v1 transport. iOS returns an APNs token, so WP-22 must add platform-aware storage and an APNs adapter/router before iOS push can be called live. The member notification and device-registration HTTP routes are also still Phase 2 work; this is a verified client contract, not a server-delivery claim. Permission denial remains non-prompting at authenticated startup and Android channel recreation remains independent of first consent. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, `expo install --check`, and **240/240** Jest assertions across 20 suites pass; the resolved Expo config still proves SDK 57, `altaros`, version 1.0.0, `com.altaros.app`, and the notification/SecureStore plugins. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-native-push-token-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Expo Doctor remains **19/20** only because historical `.expo` deletions are not yet recorded in Git. Native push receipt QA and WP-19's real-phone SMS/Paystack run remain open.

**Offline payment-return safety follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** a Paystack callback opened while definitively offline previously invoked settlement anyway, consumed one of the bounded verification attempts, collapsed the result into a generic provider failure, and left “Check again” actionable even though the API interceptor could only reject it. Payment completion now performs an explicit preflight before incrementing attempts: invalid callbacks and incomplete sessions retain their authoritative messages, while an offline callback states that payment status is unknown, warns against starting another charge, disables manual verification with an accessible reconnect explanation, and automatically verifies when connectivity returns. The same audit found event-detail RSVP still writable offline even though list-card RSVP was already protected; both event entry points now share the visible disabled/reconnect behavior while read-only details and Maps remain available. Regression tests prove offline settlement deferral and preflight priority. Mobile ESLint, TypeScript, `git diff --check`, `expo install --check`, and **242/242** Jest assertions across 20 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-offline-payment-return-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. This does not claim provider success: WP-19 remains open for a real-phone Paystack charge/callback/webhook, OTP, and SMS receipt run.

**Member RSVP restoration follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the live legacy event router declared `GET /events/:id` before `GET /events/rsvps/me`. Express therefore captured the RSVP-history request as an event whose identifier was literally `rsvps`; the mobile client deliberately tolerated that secondary lookup failure and consequently rendered every event as not attending after reload. The fixed member route is now registered before the dynamic detail route, and a regression inspects the real Express router stack so a future reorder cannot silently shadow it again. Existing controller tenancy checks still hide another church's detail or RSVP target as not found, while the mobile list continues to validate church ownership defensively. The complete legacy API suite passes **14/14** assertions across 4 files, its TypeScript build passes, and ESLint has zero errors with the same seven explicit console warnings. Mobile TypeScript, ESLint, `git diff --check`, `expo install --check`, and **242/242** Jest assertions across 20 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-rsvp-route-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Event attendance restoration is locally verified; WP-19's real-phone Paystack/SMS gate remains open.

**Session reconciliation race follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** foreground and connectivity recovery coalesced `/auth/me`, but a response already in flight could complete after local sign-out and unconditionally commit the old member again. That race remounted the authenticated navigator after credentials had begun clearing; the same stale-write path existed when profile pull-to-refresh overlapped logout. Session identity now carries a monotonically increasing revision. Reconciliation and manual profile refresh may commit only when their starting revision is still current *and* a local session still exists; password login, OTP verification, token expiry, local logout, and global logout advance the revision, while a current authoritative 401/403 advances it before clearing secure state. Stale successes and stale terminal failures therefore cannot overwrite a newer login or resurrect an ended session. Regression cases cover current, older, future, sessionless, negative, and fractional revisions. Mobile TypeScript, ESLint, `git diff --check`, `expo install --check`, and **248/248** Jest assertions across 20 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-session-revision-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. WP-19 remains open for the real-phone OTP/SMS and Paystack staging proof.

**Global logout truthfulness follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** “Sign out on all devices” previously started local credential deletion concurrently with `/auth/logout-all`, removed the authenticated UI before the gateway answered, and swallowed revocation failure. A member offline or behind a failed request was therefore told by the resulting signed-out screen that every session had ended even though other devices remained active and this phone could no longer retry. Global logout now keeps the current session until the gateway confirms token-family revocation, exposes a busy state, disables the action while definitively offline with an accessible reconnect explanation, and presents a retryable error if confirmation fails. After confirmation it clears only when the captured token is still current; a newer OTP/password login is never erased by an older in-flight revocation. Local single-device logout intentionally retains its fail-closed immediate-clear behavior. Regression cases prove confirmation-before-clear, failed-revocation preservation, successful cleanup, and newer-token protection. Mobile TypeScript, ESLint, `git diff --check`, `expo install --check`, and **251/251** Jest assertions across 20 suites pass; `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-global-logout-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Native QA was checked again against the currently booted iOS 18.5 iPhone 16 Pro simulator, but neither Expo Go nor an Altar OS build is installed, so no interactive simulator claim is made. WP-19 remains open for the real-phone OTP/SMS and Paystack staging proof.

**Latest-read ownership follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** replace-style screen reads had no response ownership. A slow focus load could finish after pull-to-refresh, an old church/member request could finish after identity changed, and a blurred screen could later overwrite the next focused view; whichever response arrived last won even when it was the oldest request. One shared monotonic request gate now gives only the newest read permission to commit data, errors, or loading flags, and focus/effect cleanup invalidates work whose screen no longer owns the view. It covers home’s parallel event/devotional/sermon load, events and event detail, giving history, notifications, community feed and comments, devotionals, sermons, prayer requests, and private welfare history. Mutation submission locks remain separate because writes must not be silently superseded. Regression cases prove overlap ordering, lifecycle invalidation, and malformed ticket rejection. These changes harden Phase 2 clients but do not claim that social, spiritual, welfare, or member-notification HTTP ownership has moved ahead of WP-19. Mobile TypeScript and ESLint pass without warnings, `git diff --check` and `expo install --check` pass, and **257/257** Jest assertions across 21 suites pass. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-latest-read-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. WP-19 remains open for the real-phone OTP/SMS and Paystack staging proof.

**Mutation/refresh reconciliation follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** protecting replace-style reads exposed the inverse race: a successful write could finish after a newer refresh. Comments, welfare cases, or prayer requests could be inserted twice if the refreshed list already contained the returned identifier; an RSVP confirmation could increment attendance twice if refresh had already restored `isRsvped`; a count-less prayer acknowledgement could add again after another response advanced the count; and a failed optimistic reaction restored an entire stale post snapshot, erasing newer comment counts or content. Shared reconciliation primitives now make returned inserts idempotent by identifier, adjust boolean-backed counts only when current state has not already reached the confirmed target, preserve a newer advanced count when no authoritative count is returned, and roll back only the exact optimistic reaction fields it owns. They are applied to feed reactions, comments, event-list/detail RSVP, prayer creation/acknowledgement, and private welfare creation. Regression cases cover duplicate insertion, already-confirmed toggles, authoritative counts, refresh-safe rollback, and increment deduplication. Mobile TypeScript and ESLint pass without warnings, `git diff --check` and `expo install --check` pass, and **261/261** Jest assertions across 22 suites pass. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-mutation-reconciliation-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Phase 2 server ownership remains unclaimed and WP-19 remains open for the real-phone OTP/SMS and Paystack staging proof.

**Deep-linked event tenant boundary follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the live event list already required every item to echo the signed-in church, and the legacy controller independently hides cross-church detail requests, but the mobile detail client normalized an event without passing its expected tenant. A compromised or misrouted proxy response could therefore mount another church's event when reached directly from `altaros://events/:eventId`, outside the list boundary. Event detail now requires the current member church, rejects a mismatched response before requesting RSVP state, and reload ownership follows church identity as well as the event identifier. A focused accessibility audit also confirmed every custom `TouchableOpacity` in mobile screens/components carries an explicit role; existing labels, visible text, selected/checked/busy/disabled states remain intact. The new regression proves a cross-tenant detail is rejected and causes no secondary RSVP request. Mobile TypeScript and ESLint pass without warnings, `git diff --check` and `expo install --check` pass, and **262/262** Jest assertions across 22 suites pass. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-event-detail-tenant-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. WP-19 remains open for real-phone OTP/SMS and Paystack staging proof.

**Exact member-history follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** `/finance/me/giving` is capped at 500 rows and each transaction already required exact safe-integer minor units, but the client summed confirmed rows with ordinary `number` addition. Enough individually valid values could cross `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER` and render a rounded lifetime total. History now accumulates confirmed minor units with `BigInt`, excludes unresolved rows from that summary, and refuses a result that cannot be converted back exactly for `Intl.NumberFormat`. The same boundary now requires the signed-in church/member, permits anonymous rows only when `memberId` is intentionally omitted by the private owner query, and rejects another tenant, another attributed member, or duplicate transaction identifiers before `FlatList` and the summary mount. Regression cases prove exact confirmed-only summation, overflow rejection, valid owner history, and duplicate/cross-owner refusal. Mobile TypeScript and ESLint pass without warnings, `git diff --check` and `expo install --check` pass, and **265/265** Jest assertions across 22 suites pass. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-exact-history-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. This strengthens local/accounting correctness but does not replace WP-19's real-phone Paystack charge, webhook, settlement, and SMS receipt proof; that gate remains open.

**Canonical production-origin follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the deployment plan names `https://api.altaros.com/api/v1`, but an unset mobile environment silently fell back to the unrelated hyphenated host `api.altar-os.com`; the profile help and privacy links had the same hostname error. Mobile now falls back to the canonical API origin and validates every configured origin before the API client mounts: production requires HTTPS, development may deliberately use HTTP for localhost/LAN testing, the path must be exactly `/api/v1`, and credentials, query strings, fragments, control characters, malformed URLs, and oversized values are refused. The example environment documents the validated production value, while help and privacy now use `https://altaros.com`. Regression cases cover production/development defaults, staging canonicalization, development LAN access, and every rejected URL class. Mobile TypeScript and ESLint pass without warnings, `git diff --check` and `expo install --check` pass, and **274/274** Jest assertions across 23 suites pass. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-api-origin-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles; all three contain the canonical API and support origins, and none contains either stale hyphenated hostname. This proves build-time client configuration, not public DNS or provider availability; WP-19 remains open for real-phone Paystack, webhook, OTP/SMS, and receipt proof.

**Native-push runtime boundary follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the Expo web bundle displayed the same “Enable notifications” action as installed iOS/Android builds even though this client deliberately acquires native APNs/FCM tokens and the backend has no browser-push registration contract. A web member could therefore grant browser permission and only then receive an invalid-token failure. Call-site guards initially prevented the bad action, but a live Expo web start exposed that merely importing `expo-notifications` installs a native push-token listener and emits an unsupported-runtime warning before those guards run. Notification access now passes through Expo platform files: iOS/Android retain the real module, while web receives a deliberately inert adapter and never bundles the native notification runtime. Authenticated web startup does not inspect notification permission or attach notification-response listeners, the inbox explains that push alerts require the installed app, and no channel creation, permission prompt, device-token read, or registration request can run there. Web deep links continue through ordinary URL linking; native builds retain their consent-first token, foreground presentation, church channel, and notification-response paths. A responsive browser QA attempt found no controllable browser attached to this session, so no new visual evidence is claimed; native device QA, the WP-22 iOS APNs/server routing work, and WP-19's real-phone Paystack/SMS run remain open.

**Offline read-state follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the request boundary already stops every Axios call before transport when NetInfo definitively reports offline, and session recovery correctly keeps a validated cached member mounted through that failure, but Home, Giving History, Devotional, and Sermons discarded the typed offline error and rendered unrelated service-failure copy. Their pull-to-refresh and retry controls also stayed actionable, producing a spinner followed by the same guaranteed failure. Those four core read surfaces now preserve the explicit reconnect message, suppress pull-to-refresh while definitively offline, and disable retry controls with visible and accessibility-specific reconnect guidance. Unknown reachability remains permissive, so a slow or unavailable reachability probe cannot falsely lock the app. A focused regression proves only the typed offline condition receives offline copy and unrelated internal errors remain behind safe surface-specific fallbacks. Mobile TypeScript and ESLint pass without warnings, `git diff --check` and `expo install --check` pass, and **277/277** Jest assertions across 23 suites pass. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-offline-read-states-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. This is truthful degraded-network UX, not WP-45 offline content sync; native visual QA and WP-19's real-phone Paystack/SMS staging gate remain open.

**Session-token integrity follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** login, OTP verification, refresh rotation, and stored-session recovery previously treated any non-empty string as a credential. A response or corrupted legacy/envelope value containing whitespace or control characters could therefore reach an `Authorization` header, while an unbounded value could be written into the device keychain and repeatedly fail recovery; identical access/refresh values also defeated the rotating-pair invariant. One shared credential boundary now requires two distinct, non-empty visible-ASCII tokens of at most 4,096 bytes each without trimming or rewriting their identity. It runs before login/OTP results are accepted, before a refresh rotation overwrites the current family, before every secure-envelope write/read, and before a legacy pair is migrated. Invalid stored or legacy material is deleted and treated as signed out; invalid issued/rotated material is refused before persistence. Failure-injection cases cover control characters, surrounding/internal whitespace, DEL, oversized values, identical pairs, unsafe secure envelopes, unsafe legacy migration, and unsafe refresh responses. Mobile TypeScript and ESLint pass without warnings, `git diff --check` and `expo install --check` pass, and **291/291** Jest assertions across 24 suites pass. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-session-token-boundary-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. This proves local credential integrity and recovery behavior; native visual QA and WP-19's live-phone Paystack/SMS staging gate remain open.

**Native simulator QA follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the previously stalled official Expo Go download eventually completed, installing Expo Go **57.0.5** on the booted iOS 18.5 iPhone 16 Pro simulator. The Altar OS native bundle compiled **1,196 modules in 6.8s** and mounted without an application runtime error. `/tmp/altar-mobile-native-login-20260801.png` is a real 1,206 × 2,622 simulator framebuffer capture, not a web emulation: the visible member-login hero, brand mark, status-bar safe area, editorial heading, support copy, and rounded form-sheet edge all fit the 402-point portrait viewport without horizontal clipping. Expo Go's own first-launch developer-menu tutorial covered the lower authentication form, and the computer-control accessibility bridge returned `cgWindowNotFound` for the running Simulator window, so no tap, keyboard, OTP, scroll, or full-form visual claim is made from this run. Expo Go also emits its expected SDK 53+ warning that remote notifications require a development build; notification receipt QA therefore remains a native-development-build/provider task. This advances native runtime and upper-auth responsive evidence from missing to partial; unobstructed full-flow/keyboard/accessibility QA, WP-22 native notification delivery, and WP-19's real-phone Paystack/SMS staging gate remain open.

**Unobstructed native login QA follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** Expo Go's internal dev-menu preference keys were set only in the disposable simulator to complete its one-time tutorial and hide its floating debug button, then the same project was relaunched without changing Altar OS code or data. `/tmp/altar-mobile-native-login-clean-20260801.png` is a clean 1,206 × 2,622 iOS framebuffer capture of the complete signed-out phone-code screen. Visual inspection confirms the top and bottom safe areas, brand/hero hierarchy, rounded form-sheet transition, phone/password mode switcher, labelled mobile field, primary action, account-creation link, and secure-storage note all fit the 402 × 874-point viewport with no overlap, truncation, horizontal clipping, or unintended content loss. The relaunch rebundled in **44ms** and logged no Altar OS runtime error; only Expo Go's already-documented notification limitation warning remained. **Platform-truthful session copy follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** native session storage uses Expo SecureStore with this-device-only accessibility, while the web fallback necessarily uses browser AsyncStorage. Login already warned web members to sign out of shared browsers, but Profile still labelled every runtime “Secured on device” and global logout said “including this phone” even on web. One tested platform-copy boundary now keeps the native secure-credential statement and “Secure device storage” label only as the native presentation choice, uses “Browser session” plus shared-browser guidance on web, and calls the current endpoint a device in cross-platform logout copy. Regression cases prove web copy never promises security or encryption, native copy retains the SecureStore explanation, and every non-web Expo platform maps to the native contract. Native interaction QA, WP-22 delivery, and WP-19's real-phone Paystack/SMS gate remain open.

**Member-identity storage boundary follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** token validation still left the profile beside those tokens effectively unbounded. A malformed current-user/login response or corrupted cached profile could carry control characters into headings/accessibility labels, place oversized values into SecureStore, mount opaque IDs that cannot safely address routes, or trigger an automatic avatar fetch over cleartext HTTP or embedded credentials. Member normalization now bounds and sanitizes every identity field before private navigation mounts: IDs use a 128-character safe opaque grammar, role remains allowlisted, names are capped at the gateway's 120-character contract, email/phone/church display fields are bounded and control-character-free, and avatar candidates survive only as credential-free HTTPS URLs no longer than 2,048 characters. Invalid avatars fall back to initials without invalidating an otherwise valid login. The cached profile itself is capped at 16 KiB before write and before JSON parsing; oversized cache material is deleted and recovered through the existing token-backed identity path. Regression cases cover unsafe IDs, control-bearing names, oversized church names, HTTP versus HTTPS avatars, oversized profile writes, and oversized stored-profile cleanup. Mobile TypeScript and ESLint pass without warnings, `git diff --check` and `expo install --check` pass, and **300/300** Jest assertions across 25 suites pass. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-member-identity-boundary-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Native interaction QA, WP-22 delivery, and WP-19's real-phone Paystack/SMS gate remain open.

**Community collection boundary follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** church ownership, safe totals, and duplicate identifiers already protected the community feed, but a malformed or compromised response could still return more records than requested or carry unbounded post/comment text, author labels, identifiers, dates, and media strings into `FlatList`, accessibility labels, and automatic image fetches. Feed and comment pages now accept only positive safe pagination within the 100-row client ceiling and reject over-limit response arrays before normalization. Community IDs use the same bounded 128-character opaque grammar as member identity; post/comment copy matches the existing 500-character composer contract; author names match the gateway's 120-character member-name contract; dates are bounded before parsing; and optional avatars/images must be credential-free HTTPS URLs no longer than 2,048 characters. Create-post and add-comment inputs pass the same checks before transport, so service callers cannot bypass screen `maxLength` controls. Regression cases cover oversized text and author names, unsafe IDs, credential-bearing media, excess server rows, malformed pagination with no request, and oversized comment submission with no mutation. Mobile TypeScript and ESLint pass without warnings, `git diff --check` and `expo install --check` pass, and **307/307** Jest assertions across 25 suites pass. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-social-collection-boundary-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. This is client collection hardening only; Phase 2 social-service ownership remains unclaimed and WP-19 stays open for the real-phone Paystack/SMS staging gate.

**Notification collection boundary follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** notification ownership, enum/date validation, route allowlisting, and duplicate identifiers were already enforced, but the client requests the complete inbox and each row concatenates its title and body into one assistive label. A malformed future service response could therefore place an unlimited record set or extremely large text into `FlatList`, visual layout, and screen-reader announcements. The inbox now refuses more than 200 rows before normalization. Notification IDs use the bounded 128-character opaque grammar; titles are capped at 200 characters without control characters; bodies are capped at 4,096 characters, preserve ordinary tabs/newlines for legitimate formatted messages, and reject other controls; date strings are capped before parsing. Existing deep-link metadata remains reduced to the strict in-app route allowlist and is not retained in list state. The preceding community boundary was corrected in the same pass to preserve ordinary line breaks in valid 500-character post/comment copy while still rejecting unsafe controls. Regression cases cover unsafe IDs, oversized title/body, control injection, excess inbox rows, and legitimate multiline notification/community content. Mobile TypeScript and ESLint pass without warnings, `git diff --check` and `expo install --check` pass, and **314/314** Jest assertions across 25 suites pass. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-notification-collection-boundary-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. **Private welfare collection boundary follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** welfare cases already required exact church/member ownership, known workflow enums, valid dates, and unique IDs, but the Phase 2 shared type defines no content or retrieval bounds and there is not yet a server implementation to supply them. Mobile therefore accepted unlimited sensitive description/emergency text and mapped an unlimited private history directly inside a `ScrollView`. One explicit client contract now caps pastoral context at 2,000 characters, preserves ordinary multiline detail, rejects unsafe controls, and displays a live accessible count beside the form. The same constraint runs independently before request creation and emergency-alert transport, so alternate callers cannot bypass the input's native `maxLength`. Returned cases require bounded opaque IDs and date strings, and more than 100 private history rows fail into the existing unavailable/retry state before normalization or rendering. **Live event collection boundary follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** events are currently the only post-core member collection reachable through the gateway proxy, and the legacy controller already caps `limit` at 100 while defaulting to 20. Mobile sent caller pagination unchecked, accepted any response length, forwarded every returned ID into the RSVP-history query, and allowed unbounded event copy/dates into list/detail rendering. The client now validates positive safe page/limit values, boolean upcoming state, and sort direction before transport; it rejects a response larger than the exact requested/default page before normalization or RSVP lookup. Event IDs and church owners are capped at 128 control-free characters, dates at 64 characters before parsing, titles at 200, locations at 300, and multiline descriptions at 5,000 while unsafe controls are refused. RSVP lookup accepts at most the already-bounded event set, requires requested IDs, and rejects excess or duplicate history entries internally; detail and RSVP mutations also reject unsafe/oversized IDs before transport. Home's nearest-event helper now accepts only 1–50 requested results. Regression cases cover every field bound, multiline description retention, invalid pagination/no request, over-limit response/no secondary RSVP request, unsafe home count, and mutation ID refusal. **Spiritual collection and prayer-input boundary follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** devotional, sermon, and prayer records already required exact church ownership, known core fields, sane dates/counts, and unique IDs, but pages and long-form content were unbounded, media validation happened only when a member tapped playback, and prayer creation relied entirely on an unconstrained screen input. All spiritual list calls now validate positive safe pagination with a 100-row ceiling and 20-row default, refuse responses above the exact requested page before normalization, and bound optional sermon-series filters. IDs are capped at 128 control-free characters; titles at 200; names at 120; date/duration/reference/series fields receive narrow limits; devotional scripture/content retain up to 50,000 multiline characters; sermon descriptions retain 5,000; prayer descriptions retain 2,000. Prayer title/detail limits are visible through native input bounds and an accessible remaining-character count, while create/pray service calls independently reject invalid content or IDs before POST. Regression cases cover unsafe/oversized prayer fields, multiline retention, invalid pagination/no request, excessive response pages, oversized mutation IDs, and oversized media URLs. **Offline read-control completion follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** Home, Giving History, Devotionals, and Sermons already suppressed guaranteed-failure refresh/retry actions when NetInfo definitively reported offline, but Events, Event Detail, Notifications, Profile, Community Feed/Comments, Prayer, and Welfare still allowed pull-to-refresh or retry despite sharing the same request interceptor and offline hook. Every mobile `RefreshControl` now uses one conservative policy: a definitive offline signal disables the gesture and its callback guard, while unknown reachability remains usable. All remaining visible retry controls expose disabled accessibility state plus screen-specific reconnect guidance; Event Detail also replaces generic connection copy with an explicit offline explanation. Events and Community Feed had a separate empty-error dead end—when no cached rows existed they displayed failure copy without any retry—so both now provide the same reconnect-aware retry available in their cached-data banners. A proposed source-scanning invariant test was run successfully but intentionally removed because the Expo TypeScript project excludes Node globals and should not acquire Node ambient types merely for a filesystem test; the existing connectivity-policy tests prove definitive-versus-unknown behavior, while current source audit proves all ten refresh surfaces apply it. Mobile TypeScript and ESLint pass without warnings, `git diff --check` and `expo install --check` pass, and **337/337** Jest assertions across 25 suites pass. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-offline-read-controls-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Flight-mode native interaction QA and WP-19's real-phone Paystack/SMS staging gate remain open.

**Deep-link query integrity follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** route paths and dynamic event/community IDs were already allowlisted, and their screens/services revalidated IDs and finance references, but the navigation boundary stripped query/fragment data before checking the path. That made arbitrary queries valid on every static or dynamic route, including profile and event destinations. Only the Paystack return route needs query data. Deep links now reject fragments universally and reject any query outside `giving/complete`. Payment callbacks accept only unique `reference` and/or `trxref` keys, at most one of each, and every value must match the finance service's exact case-insensitive `alt_` plus 32-character base32 reference grammar before Navigation or deferred-auth storage receives it. The supported query-less payment-status route remains available for controlled in-app navigation, while malformed, duplicated, foreign-key, injected, or oversized callbacks fail closed. Regression cases cover canonical single/dual Paystack parameters, queries on profile and dynamic event routes, duplicate keys, script-like references, and fragments; the deferred-auth test now uses a production-shaped finance reference rather than its earlier abbreviated fixture. Mobile TypeScript and ESLint pass without warnings, `git diff --check` and `expo install --check` pass, and **338/338** Jest assertions across 25 suites pass. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-route-query-boundary-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. This is deterministic route-boundary evidence, not a live provider return; WP-19 stays open for the real-phone Paystack/SMS staging gate.

**Authentication input and church-enrichment boundary follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** issued-session/user normalization was strict, but signed-out inputs were not aligned with the Go gateway before transport. Login and registration fields had no native maximums; the auth service forwarded arbitrary login method/payload shapes; registration did not mirror the gateway's 120-byte combined-name or bcrypt 8–72-byte limits; OTP verification accepted any string when called outside the six-box screen; formatted phone input and church display responses were effectively unbounded. All login/register inputs now expose matching native caps: names 120 characters each (with the service enforcing the combined UTF-8 byte limit), email 254, formatted phone 32, password 72, and church code 80. The service canonicalizes and validates email, accepts only password-login semantics with a non-empty control-free password no larger than 72 UTF-8 bytes, and sends one canonical payload. Registration validates the combined control-free name, canonical email, phone, and the gateway's exact 8–72 UTF-8-byte password range before church lookup or POST; the user-facing error explains that some symbols use multiple bytes. OTP exchange independently requires exactly six digits. Current/registration church IDs now pass the same 128-character identity grammar, display names are capped at 160, and slugs at 80 before enrichment can enter session/profile state. Regression cases cover oversized formatted phones, normalized password login, malformed/oversized login with no POST, multibyte bcrypt overflow with no lookup/POST, malformed OTP with no POST, and oversized church display data. Mobile TypeScript and ESLint pass without warnings, `git diff --check` and `expo install --check` pass, and **343/343** Jest assertions across 25 suites pass. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-auth-input-boundary-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. This proves deterministic auth boundaries; live SMS receipt and device OTP remain inside WP-19, which stays open.

**Giving-intent transport boundary follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the giving screen constrained the member's visible choices, but the financial service still trusted TypeScript types at runtime and would post any alternate caller's malformed currency, giving type, payment channel, email, note, callback, or accepted debit total. Quote and checkout now share one pre-transport intent boundary: amounts are converted to the exact two-decimal representation, currency is exactly GHS, charge channels exclude cash, anonymous state is explicitly boolean, giving types remain allowlisted, email is canonical and bounded, and optional notes are trimmed, control-free, and capped at the screen's 240-character contract. Checkout accepts only the app's exact payment-return URL and a positive safe-integer consent total that cannot be below the gift itself; incomplete member identity is also refused before POST. The canonical request—not the caller's original object—is the only payload sent and the same request is used to verify the returned transaction. Regression cases prove normalization and no-transport refusal for unknown types, cash checkout, foreign currency, malformed email, oversized/control-bearing notes, foreign callbacks, unsafe/fractional/under-gift consent totals, and malformed quote channels. Mobile TypeScript and ESLint pass without warnings, `git diff --check` and `expo install --check` pass, and **355/355** Jest assertions across 25 suites pass. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-giving-intent-boundary-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. This closes the deterministic member-intent edge only; WP-19 remains open for real-phone Paystack settlement, webhook, OTP/SMS, and receipt proof.

**Giving-history query and bounded-ledger follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the self-history endpoint intentionally converts an unparseable `from` or `to` value to an empty bound, so an unchecked malformed mobile filter silently widened the requested financial window. The client also trusted the server's 500-row implementation ceiling and accepted unbounded transaction identifiers, provider references, dates, and notes into ledger state. History filters now accept only real calendar dates in the gateway's exact `YYYY-MM-DD` or RFC3339 forms, reject inverted ranges, and fail before GET rather than widening the query. Responses over 500 rows fail before normalization. Transaction, church, member, idempotency, and optional provider identifiers share a 128-character safe opaque grammar; dates are bounded before parsing; notes retain legitimate multiline text within 240 characters while unsafe controls are refused; and net proceeds cannot exceed the gross gift. The same member-identity grammar now protects the history and callback service entry points. Regression cases prove valid date-only/RFC3339 ranges, empty-filter canonicalization, impossible calendar dates, local timestamps, inverted ranges, no-request rejection, the 501st-row ceiling, unsafe/oversized record fields, and impossible net amounts. Mobile TypeScript and ESLint pass without warnings, `git diff --check` and `expo install --check` pass, and **372/372** Jest assertions across 25 suites pass. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-giving-history-boundary-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. This strengthens deterministic ledger integrity only; WP-19 remains open for real-phone Paystack settlement, webhook, OTP/SMS, and receipt proof.

**Checkout-response debit integrity follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** checkout already matched the returned church, owner, gift type, channel, currency, gross amount, pending state, accepted total, and allowlisted Paystack URL, but it did not prove that the levy stored on the pending ledger row matched the accepted quote. It also accepted an arbitrary idempotency key even though only the finance service's `alt_` plus 32-character base32 reference can survive the app's payment-return route. Checkout normalization now requires the transaction levy to equal the quote levy, the pending row to carry an exact callback-safe finance reference, no premature provider settlement reference, and the same normalized note the member submitted. Quote totals are compared with `BigInt` rather than potentially overflowing JavaScript addition; quote reasons are non-empty, control-free, and capped at 500 characters; provider access codes are control-free and capped at 512. Regression cases reject levy disagreement, a foreign reference shape, premature provider reference, changed note, oversized/control-bearing access code, and oversized/control-bearing quote reason. Mobile TypeScript and ESLint pass without warnings, `git diff --check` and `expo install --check` pass, and **380/380** Jest assertions across 25 suites pass. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-checkout-response-integrity-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. This proves deterministic response binding before opening Paystack; WP-19 remains open for real-phone settlement, webhook, OTP/SMS, and receipt proof.

**Focus-refresh recovery and offline-copy follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the latest-request gate correctly invalidated Home, Community Feed, and Post Comments reads when their screens lost focus, but all three are focus-owned mounted screens and only the still-current request cleared the native refresh flag. Leaving during pull-to-refresh therefore invalidated its completion, and the next ordinary focus load did not reset `refreshing`; returning could leave a spinner stuck indefinitely. Every screen using `useFocusEffect` plus the request gate now explicitly begins an ordinary focus load with refresh state false, while an actual pull begins true and only its current completion ends it. Home also appended “Pull down to try again” after the typed offline error even though offline policy disables that gesture. Event and sermon fallbacks now contain retry guidance only for non-offline failures; typed reconnect copy remains standalone. Feed, Comments, and Profile refreshes likewise preserve the shared known-offline message instead of replacing it with a generic service error. A source audit confirms these are the only three focus-owned gated refresh screens; the other gated surfaces unmount rather than persist across navigation. Mobile TypeScript and ESLint pass without warnings, `git diff --check` and `expo install --check` pass, and the unchanged **380/380** Jest assertions across 25 suites pass, including latest-request invalidation and definitive-versus-unknown connectivity policy. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-focus-refresh-recovery-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. Interactive blur-during-refresh evidence still requires controllable native navigation; WP-19 remains open for real-phone Paystack/SMS staging proof.

**Notification read-race and action-integrity follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** inbox rows were normalized through the bounded safe-ID grammar, but the read mutation itself accepted any caller string and encoded it into the route, while list ownership inputs were not independently checked before transport. Both now fail before a request unless they are bounded opaque identifiers. Optimistic read rollback previously restored the captured unread value unconditionally, so a list refresh that committed a newer authoritative READ timestamp while the mutation was failing could be erased by the older catch handler. Rollback now changes only the exact optimistic timestamp that request still owns; a successful mutation also restores its timestamp only when a concurrent list response temporarily returned the row unread. A mark-read network race no longer returns before opening an already-allowlisted local destination. Offline unread rows with such a destination remain navigable with truthful “mark later” assistive copy; an offline unread row without a destination is disabled instead of advertising a no-op mark-read action, and already-read destination-free rows are likewise non-actionable. Push enablement now exposes a visible busy label and matching disabled/busy accessibility state, and inbox loads preserve typed reconnect copy. Regression cases prove no-transport refusal for unsafe notification/member identifiers and ownership-safe timestamp rollback. Mobile TypeScript and ESLint pass without warnings, `git diff --check` and `expo install --check` pass, and **383/383** Jest assertions across 25 suites pass. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-notification-read-integrity-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. This is client action integrity for the Phase 2 contract, not live notification HTTP or FCM/APNs proof; WP-19 remains open for real-phone Paystack/SMS staging evidence.

**RSVP member-ownership boundary follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the live shared RSVP object contains its own ID, event ID, authenticated member ID, and status, but mobile discarded the row/member identities and accepted a mutation result solely because its event and requested status matched. The `/events/rsvps/me` adapter likewise allowed an unowned same-event GOING row to set `isRsvped`. Event list, detail, Home, confirm, and cancel now thread the normalized session member ID through the service. Every live RSVP row must carry a bounded safe row ID, exact requested event ID, exact authenticated member ID, and an allowlisted status; confirm/cancel additionally require the exact requested status. A foreign or malformed self-history row is rejected inside the optional history lookup and therefore cannot mark the member as attending, while a foreign mutation result fails the action. Event, church, and member route inputs now share the bounded opaque identifier grammar and fail before transport instead of encoding slashes/spaces into Mongo-backed routes. Regression cases cover unsafe event/church/member no-request refusal, foreign mutation ownership, foreign self-history isolation, and the valid full shared response. Mobile TypeScript and ESLint pass without warnings, `git diff --check` and `expo install --check` pass, and **386/386** Jest assertions across 25 suites pass. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-rsvp-ownership-boundary-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. The legacy service still does not enforce capacity atomically, so WP-21's server-side oversubscription acceptance remains open; WP-19 remains open for real-phone Paystack/SMS staging proof.

**Community mutation-intent boundary follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** feed and comment records already enforced church/post ownership and bounded content, but mutation acknowledgements did not prove that a newly created post/comment belonged to the submitting member or preserved the content/type they submitted. Create Post and Post Comments now pass the normalized session member ID into the service. A created post must match the expected church, author, canonical trimmed content, and selected allowlisted type; a created comment must match its target post, author, and canonical trimmed content. The post request is rebuilt from the allowlisted `content`, `type`, and optional safe image URL rather than spreading a caller object that could smuggle extra fields. Existing keyed reaction locks and ownership-aware optimistic rollback remain intact. Regression cases cover foreign authors, server-edited post/comment content, changed post type, stripped injected request fields, and no-request refusal across all unsafe community route/identity inputs. Mobile TypeScript and ESLint pass without warnings, `git diff --check` and `expo install --check` pass, and **396/396** Jest assertions across 25 suites pass. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-community-mutation-integrity-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. **Prayer privacy-minimization and mutation-intent follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** anonymous prayer cards hid the author's name visually but the normalized public-list object still retained both `memberId` and `authorName` in application state, preserving identity data the member explicitly chose not to expose. Public prayer normalization now reconstructs a minimal object and omits member ownership universally from community-list state; it also omits the author label whenever `isAnonymous` is true. **Welfare privacy-minimization and mutation-intent follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the private welfare adapter already checked that returned cases belonged to the signed-in member and church, but it accepted a newly created case even when the server changed the submitted category, description, urgency, anonymity choice, or initial status. Creation now canonicalises the description, rebuilds the request from exactly four allowlisted fields, and requires the owned acknowledgement to preserve every submitted value and begin in `pending`. History and creation reject unsafe tenant/member identifiers before transport. Member-facing normalization now reconstructs only the declared case contract rather than casting and retaining server-only fields such as assignments, staff notes, or risk metadata in application state. Welfare history failures preserve the shared typed reconnect message. **Authentication intent-binding follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** valid token and user shapes were accepted without proving that the returned member was the credential holder who had just authenticated. Password login now requires the canonical returned email to equal the submitted email, and OTP verification requires the canonical returned phone to equal the number that received the code; either mismatch fails before church enrichment, token persistence, or cached-user persistence. The OTP request body is rebuilt from exactly `otp` and canonical `phone`, preventing runtime-added fields from crossing the boundary. Registration acknowledgement now binds church, canonical phone, canonical email, full name, and `MEMBER` role to the submitted identity before the unverified account is accepted into the verification journey. Returned and cached email/phone fields are canonicalised and malformed present values fail instead of entering private navigation. Regression cases prove foreign login/OTP responses create no local session, registration email/name drift fails, injected OTP fields are stripped, and formatted identities are stored canonically. **Session-family race hardening follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** refresh requests were single-flight, but their eventual storage mutation was unconditional. A rotation started under an old account could therefore finish after logout and resurrect that token family, or finish after a newer login and overwrite the new member's credentials. An authoritative 401 from that stale refresh could likewise clear the newer login. Token writes, logout cleanup, conditional rotation, and conditional expiry now share one serialized mutation lane. Successful rotation uses compare-and-swap on the exact refresh token that initiated it; a stale result is rejected and its original authenticated request is never replayed under the replacement member. Refresh rejection clears and notifies expiry only when that same token family is still current. Legacy-pair migration writes through the same internal atomic envelope path without deadlocking the mutation lane. Regression cases prove slow older writes cannot overtake newer login, post-logout rotation cannot restore a session, stale success cannot replace a newer family, stale rejection cannot clear or unmount a newer member, and matching rotation/expiry still work. Mobile TypeScript and ESLint pass without warnings, `git diff --check` and `expo install --check` pass, and **429/429** Jest assertions across 25 suites pass. The first export invocation was run from the monorepo root and correctly failed entry resolution because Expo's `AppEntry` is workspace-relative; the canonical mobile-workspace invocation then succeeded, and `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-session-family-race-v2` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. WP-19 remains open for real-phone OTP/SMS and Paystack settlement/webhook/receipt proof.

**Cold-start navigation and payment-callback integrity follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** `Linking.getInitialURL()` retains the process launch URL, so reading it again after logout could defer an already-consumed event, comment, or payment destination and replay it into the next account that signed in. Payment callbacks formerly accepted individually valid `reference` and `trxref` query values even when they named different transactions, while the screen silently preferred `reference`. The deep-link allowlist now requires both aliases to match case-insensitively when both exist, and the settlement screen independently normalises both, rejects either malformed alias, and refuses disagreement before contacting finance. Regression cases prove launch URLs are read once even after a read failure/account transition, conflicting callback queries are filtered before navigation, and direct route parameters cannot select one of two different valid transactions. Checkout formerly required only non-empty church/member strings, while transaction lookup and settlement validated those identities only after transport. **Atomic guarded authentication-attempt commit follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** login and OTP services previously persisted tokens before `AuthContext` could reject a stale completion, and the token pair and cached profile were written through separate lanes. A slow abandoned sign-in could therefore finish after logout or a newer attempt and restore credentials, overwrite the replacement token family, or leave tokens and profile belonging to different members. Authenticated-session commit now validates tokens/profile up front and writes both inside the existing serialized secure-storage mutation lane. A live attempt guard is checked before storage, after token persistence, and after profile persistence; an invalidation during either device write rolls both back under the same lane before any queued newer mutation proceeds. `AuthContext` assigns a monotonic attempt revision to password and OTP authentication, invalidates it on local/global logout and authoritative expiry, and commits visible user state only for the same revision. Login and OTP screens add their own mounted guard, so backing out invalidates secure commit even though the provider remains alive; late OTP dispatch/resend navigation, alerts, errors, and busy-state writes are also suppressed after unmount. Regression coverage proves stale attempts write nothing, mid-storage invalidation removes both credentials and profile, valid atomic commit restores both, and a service refuses a structurally valid login when ownership is revoked. Mobile TypeScript and ESLint pass without warnings, `git diff --check` and `expo install --check` pass, and **506/506** Jest assertions across 34 suites pass. `/tmp/altar-mobile-export-20260801-auth-attempt-atomic-commit-v1` contains fresh web, Android, and iOS bundles. **Foreground reconciliation and durable profile-cache follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** cold start and foreground resume already reconciled the member identity with `/auth/me`, but a successful result updated only React state. The encrypted cached profile created at login was never refreshed, so later offline restarts could indefinitely restore an old name, avatar, church label, or role. Reconciled and manually refreshed profiles now update SecureStore through the same serialized session-mutation lane as token rotation and authentication. The write is conditional on both the exact initiating refresh-token family and the still-live AuthContext session revision; an old foreground request therefore cannot overwrite the cache after logout or a replacement login. A concurrent token rotation may safely skip that cache write while still accepting a guarded current identity in memory, and the next reconciliation can persist the new family. **Go notification inbox/read/device contract follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** a route-by-route comparison found that mobile called `/notifications`, `/notifications/{id}/read`, and `/notifications/devices`, but neither the Go gateway nor the legacy TypeScript router served any of them. **Push-device session-family revocation follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** native push registrations previously survived logout, so a shared or relinquished phone could continue receiving private member notifications after its local session disappeared. Device registration now requires the verified access token's session family and persists that ownership server-side. Local logout removes only registrations belonging to the exact member/session family, preserving another signed-in device; global logout removes every registration for that tenant member. Cleanup runs before session revocation on the server, eliminating the client-side unregister-versus-revoke race. A storage cleanup failure is logged and reported as `deviceCleanupConfirmed: false` while revocation still succeeds, so the app is not stranded holding an already-revoked session. Pure Go validation and response-contract regressions pass, and every Go package compiles. A Mongo-backed lifecycle regression covers idempotent registration, local-family isolation, and global removal, but is honestly **skipped in this sandbox** because local MongoDB access is denied. **Native push-token rotation follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** startup and sign-in refreshed an already-consented APNs/FCM registration, but the authenticated app never subscribed to native token-rotation events. The member navigator now owns one native rotation listener for the exact church/member identity, immediately validates and registers replacements without prompting for permission, removes the listener when that identity or authentication state changes, and checks ownership before transport so an old listener cannot register under a replacement member. Web keeps a deliberate no-op shim because browser-push delivery is not implemented. Focused regressions prove active-session rotation, cross-session rejection, unsupported-runtime behavior, and no permission/token discovery during the rotation callback. **Session-bound background request follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** the Axios request interceptor always replaced an explicitly supplied `Authorization` header with whichever access token was current when the asynchronous interceptor ran. A local or global logout could therefore revoke a replacement login instead of its initiating family, while an old push-registration request receiving 401 could refresh and replay inside that replacement family. The API client now has an explicit session-bound request contract: it preserves the captured bearer, refuses refresh/replay for that request class, and leaves ordinary foreground API calls on the existing coalesced refresh path. Local logout, global logout, startup push sync, and live token rotation all use the exact initiating access token. Push work rechecks member ownership after secure-token discovery and before transport. Pure regressions prove exact bearer capture, header preservation, refresh refusal, normal 401 refresh eligibility, and cross-session push rejection. **Device ownership-transfer and exhaustive logout follow-up — 1 Aug 2026:** a physical APNs/FCM token can survive logout and be returned by a replacement login. Because token uniqueness is tenant-wide, any failed best-effort cleanup previously made registration in the new session fail permanently; global logout also queried at most 100 rows and silently retained every registration beyond that first batch. Registration now inspects only the exact tenant-scoped token, preserves an already-correct slot, removes prior slot ownership, and then assigns the replacement member/session slot. An old-family cleanup therefore cannot remove the transferred registration. Device removal now drains repeated 100-row batches until none remain, so global logout has no cardinality ceiling.
